{"parentUuid":null,"isSidechain":true,"userType":"external","cwd":"C:\\Users\\baenb\\projects\\MIDGE","sessionId":"141c733c-7949-435d-8de2-34bf8e29a67f","version":"2.1.71","gitBranch":"main","agentId":"a7dea850a9df9eda1","slug":"delegated-leaping-map","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":"Research how MIDGE (a market intelligence system) should add geopolitical intelligence as a first-class domain for convergence detection.\n\nContext: MIDGE is a multi-domain signal convergence engine that stacks patterns across 30+ data sources (SEC filings, congressional trades, options flow, macro data, technical analysis, etc.) to detect \"inevitabilities.\" It currently has ZERO geopolitical coverage — no international news, no sanctions monitoring, no conflict tracking, no trade policy analysis. The vision is \"MIDGE sees world events before they happen.\"\n\nResearch:\n1. Free/cheap geopolitical data sources with APIs: GDELT (Global Database of Events, Language, and Tone), ACLED (Armed Conflict Location), UN Comtrade, World Bank, SIPRI arms transfers, OFAC sanctions lists, Congressional Research Service reports\n2. NLP/sentiment approaches for geopolitical text: news wire services, government press releases, UN Security Council transcripts, diplomatic cable analysis\n3. Supply chain geography: mapping which companies depend on which countries (Taiwan semiconductors, China rare earths, Middle East oil routes)\n4. Sanctions and trade policy: OFAC SDN list changes as leading indicators, tariff announcements, trade agreement negotiations\n5. Conflict and instability indices: how to quantify escalation risk and convert to market signals\n6. Historical examples: Russia-Ukraine (energy prices), China-Taiwan tensions (semiconductor stocks), OPEC decisions (oil), Suez Canal blockage (shipping)\n7. The \"second-order effects\" problem: geopolitical events don't just affect one sector — how to trace cascading impacts through supply chains\n8. Existing Python libraries for geopolitical analysis: gdeltdoc, acled, country-converter, etc.\n9. TEMPORAL LEAD: geopolitical signals often lead market reactions by days/weeks. How to measure and exploit this lag.\n\nBe specific about APIs, data formats, update frequencies, and how each source could feed into a convergence engine."},"uuid":"009aacac-4a77-4548-b807-7026a972ca9a","timestamp":"2026-03-08T12:39:35.282Z"}

{"parentUuid":"b482fefd-8e24-4516-bcd7-2548bea2ef8a","isSidechain":true,"userType":"external","cwd":"C:\\Users\\baenb\\projects\\MIDGE","sessionId":"141c733c-7949-435d-8de2-34bf8e29a67f","version":"2.1.71","gitBranch":"main","agentId":"a7dea850a9df9eda1","slug":"delegated-leaping-map","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_011jbYQHh5Sr8XLUpivWQi59","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"ACLED armed conflict location event data API Python free access\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"acled · PyPI\",\"url\":\"https://pypi.org/project/acled/\"},{\"title\":\"ACLED data\",\"url\":\"https://acleddata.com/\"},{\"title\":\"GitHub - datapartnership/acled_conflict_analysis: Python package to extract analyze and map ACLED data\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/datapartnership/acled_conflict_analysis\"},{\"title\":\"Armed Conflict Location & Event Data Project (ACLED) Humanitarian Data | 246 Datasets | HDX\",\"url\":\"https://data.humdata.org/organization/acled\"},{\"title\":\"Armed Conflict Location & Event Data Project | ReliefWeb\",\"url\":\"https://reliefweb.int/organization/acled\"},{\"title\":\"Conflict data | ACLED\",\"url\":\"https://acleddata.com/conflict-data\"},{\"title\":\"ACLED Conflict and Demonstrations Event Data\",\"url\":\"https://www.cacgeoportal.com/datasets/0ed2adbeb476430e969d5bc4261eaf06_0/about\"},{\"title\":\"Manipulate ACLED Data • acledR\",\"url\":\"https://dtacled.github.io/acledR/\"},{\"title\":\"ACLED Conflict and Demonstrations Event Data - Overview\",\"url\":\"https://www.arcgis.com/home/item.html?id=0ed2adbeb476430e969d5bc4261eaf06\"},{\"title\":\"acled.api: Automated Retrieval of ACLED Conflict Event Data\",\"url\":\"https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/acled.api/acled.api.pdf\"}]\n\nHere are the search results for ACLED API access with Python:\n\n## Python Libraries for ACLED\n\nThere is a Python library that unofficially wraps the ACLED API and provides a convenient interface for accessing and analyzing conflict and protest data from around the world. This library is available on PyPI as \"acled\" and can be installed via pip.\n\nAdditionally, there is a Python package for extracting, processing, analyzing, and visualizing conflict data from the Armed Conflict Location & Event Data Project (ACLED).\n\n## API Access Requirements\n\nAll requests to the ACLED API require authentication with a valid API key and the email that is registered to that API key, which you can obtain by registering on the ACLED website. You can create a free myACLED account to access advanced features and tools.\n\n## Data Availability\n\nThe ACLED team makes both data and analysis open for free use by the public. However, to access full event data, you need to register to use the Data Export Tool and API on the ACLED website.\n\nThe main Python package available is on PyPI at https://pypi.org/project/acled/, and requires registration with ACLED to obtain API credentials before you can start using it.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"9a067cec-ab95-4e77-97b5-0381e1d26b2f","timestamp":"2026-03-08T12:39:49.852Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"b482fefd-8e24-4516-bcd7-2548bea2ef8a"}

Complete Compliance Solutions\",\"url\":\"https://docs.ofac-api.com/datasources\"},{\"title\":\"OFAC sanctions and crypto wallet addresses | Compli API\",\"url\":\"https://compliapi.com/blog/ofac-sanctions-crypto-wallets/\"},{\"title\":\"OFAC API - Complete Compliance Solutions\",\"url\":\"https://www.ofac-api.com/\"},{\"title\":\"OFAC Sanctions List Search | OFAC Check API - Compliancely\",\"url\":\"https://compliancely.com/ofac-sanctions-list\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, I found information about OFAC SDN sanctions list API changes, though the results don't contain specific information about market impact or leading indicator analysis. Here's what I found:\n\n## OFAC SDN API Changes\n\nOFAC altered the namespace and schema location in the SDN_ADVANCED.XML and CONS_ADVANCED.XML files, which was communicated in a May 2024 notice. These technical changes involved updates to the XSD schema files to align with new URL structures.\n\n## Data Access and Formats\n\nOFAC's Sanctions List Service (SLS) provides users with easy access to the most up-to-date Sanctions Lists and Sanctions list data ready for immediate download, with users able to choose to download from either the Specially Designated Nationals (SDN) List or the Consolidated (non-SDNs) List.\n\nThe advanced sanctions data model contains additional meta data that may further aid compliance and screening programs.\n\n## Market Applications\n\nWhile specific market impact data wasn't found in these results, utilizing the OFAC API can help mitigate risks by automating the screening of crypto addresses against the latest sanction lists, and OFAC-API.com compliance APIs support a wide range of businesses and industries including Banking and FinTech, Payment Processors, and the Remittance Industry.\n\nThe search results don't contain specific information about OFAC API changes being used as a market leading indicator or their broader market impact on financial markets or trading strategies.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"eb8afb20-e80c-412e-874a-36fb5c76b0c7","timestamp":"2026-03-08T12:39:51.628Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"15be81c3-595d-47da-b17b-8afbd8303e5f"}

– The GDELT Project\",\"url\":\"https://blog.gdeltproject.org/gdelt-doc-2-0-api-debuts/\"},{\"title\":\"gdeltPyR | Python based framework to retreive Global Database of Events, Language, and Tone (GDELT) version 1.0 and version 2.0 data.\",\"url\":\"https://linwoodc3.github.io/gdeltPyR/\"},{\"title\":\"Explore Geopolitical data from GDELT - DEV Community\",\"url\":\"https://dev.to/sonishreyas/explore-geopolitical-data-from-gdelt-2hnm\"},{\"title\":\"Starting with GDELT data – EconMacro\",\"url\":\"https://www.jamelsaadaoui.com/starting-with-gdelt-data/\"},{\"title\":\"The GDELT Project\",\"url\":\"https://www.gdeltproject.org/\"},{\"title\":\"A web interface for the GDELT Project\",\"url\":\"https://gdelt.github.io/\"},{\"title\":\"GDELT Analysis Service: The Global Database of Events, Language, and Tone\",\"url\":\"https://analysis.gdeltproject.org/\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's comprehensive information about using GDELT API with Python for geopolitical events data:\n\n## GDELT Overview\n\nThe entire GDELT database is 100% free and open and you can download the raw datafiles, visualize it using the GDELT Analysis Service, or analyze it at limitless scale with Google BigQuery. The GDELT Project monitors the world's broadcast, print, and web news from nearly every corner of every country in over 100 languages and identifies the people, locations, organizations, themes, sources, emotions, counts, quotes, images and events driving our global society every second of every day, creating a free open platform for computing on the entire world.\n\n## Python Libraries for GDELT\n\n### gdeltPyR\ngdeltPyR is a Python-based framework to access and analyze Global Database of Events, Language, and Tone (GDELT) 1.0 or 2.0 data in a Python Pandas or R dataframe. gdeltPyR retrieves GDELT data, version 1.0 or version 2.0 via parallel HTTP GET requests and will provide a method to access GDELT data directly via Google BigQuery.\n\n### gdelt-doc-api\nA Python client to fetch data from the GDELT 2.0 Doc API. This allows for simpler, small-scale analysis of news coverage without having to deal with the complexities of downloading and managing the raw files from S3, or working with the BigQuery.\n\n## Data Availability & Access Methods\n\nHistorical archives stretch back to January 1, 1979 and update every 15 minutes. The API officially only supports the most recent 3 months of articles. Making a request for an earlier date range may still return data, but it's not guaranteed.\n\nThe GDELT Analysis Service is a free cloud-based service that offers a variety of tools and services to allow you to visualize, explore, and export both the GDELT Event Database and the GDELT Global Knowledge Graph.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"4f9fb054-9dce-4f92-8b8e-32fa625e41ce","timestamp":"2026-03-08T12:39:52.197Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"337b4f7f-50ef-4235-b5ed-535000b4db64"}

{"parentUuid":"1279df7a-87ef-4a06-aceb-7e2bacfb3adf","isSidechain":true,"userType":"external","cwd":"C:\\Users\\baenb\\projects\\MIDGE","sessionId":"141c733c-7949-435d-8de2-34bf8e29a67f","version":"2.1.71","gitBranch":"main","agentId":"a7dea850a9df9eda1","slug":"delegated-leaping-map","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_016sUa2BQJ5Mw2xZbdLPTtok","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"UN Comtrade trade data API Python country supply chain dependency analysis\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"GitHub - uncomtrade/comtradeapicall: Python libraries to call UN Comtrade APIs · GitHub\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/uncomtrade/comtradeapicall\"},{\"title\":\"UN Comtrade\",\"url\":\"https://comtrade.un.org/\"},{\"title\":\"Un\",\"url\":\"https://comtrade.un.org/labs/\"},{\"title\":\"API for UN Comtrade bulk data — Ka Lok Wong (Steve)\",\"url\":\"https://www.stevekwong.com/blog/apicomtrade\"},{\"title\":\"comtradeapicall · PyPI\",\"url\":\"https://pypi.org/project/comtradeapicall/\"},{\"title\":\"GitHub - KhubchandaniK/Comtrade-US: Using Comtrade data extraction API (Python)\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/KhubchandaniK/Comtrade-US\"},{\"title\":\"GitHub - ejokeeffe/tradedownloader: Download trade data from Comtrade using the exposed Comtrade API\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/ejokeeffe/tradedownloader\"},{\"title\":\"Interface with the United Nations Comtrade API • comtradr\",\"url\":\"https://docs.ropensci.org/comtradr/\"},{\"title\":\"Stata-Python API for bulk data download: Example with UN Comtrade\",\"url\":\"https://www.stata.com/meeting/switzerland22/slides/Switzerland22_Wong.pdf\"},{\"title\":\"econ_data/APIs/ComTrade.ipynb at master · bdecon/econ_data\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/bdecon/econ_data/blob/master/APIs/ComTrade.ipynb\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's what I found about using UN Comtrade trade data API with Python for supply chain dependency analysis:\n\n## Python Libraries and Tools\n\nUN Comtrade provides free and premium APIs to extract and download data, and a Python package called comtradeapicall simplifies calling these APIs. The package assumes using Python 3.7 and dependencies are listed in the \"requirements.txt\" file.\n\n## Key Features\n\nThe comtradeapicall package includes functions to extract Trade Matrix, download and combine bulk files, and calculate trade balance and bilateral data.\n\n## API Access Methods\n\nThere are multiple approaches to access Comtrade data:\n\n1. **Direct API calls**: You can fetch large amounts of data from Comtrade via Python functions by iterating through API calls. The API has a rate limit of 1 request per second, 100 requests per hour.\n\n2. **Data query parameters**: Python wrapper functions can create URLs to access the Comtrade API with parameters including year, frequency, reporter country, partner country, and commodity codes.\n\n## Supply Chain Analysis\n\nDependency analysis examples include examining a country's share of world exports (such as analyzing Ukraine's role in global cooking oil supplies) and the dependency of the world on those exports.\n\nThe available tools and documentation provide a foundation for building supply chain dependency analysis using bilateral trade data extracted through the UN Comtrade API in Python.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"ddcf02db-7c58-4956-8366-a5ce60d19760","timestamp":"2026-03-08T12:40:02.275Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"1279df7a-87ef-4a06-aceb-7e2bacfb3adf"}

{"parentUuid":"315f9998-0f90-468f-b38a-28fd43fe1a50","isSidechain":true,"userType":"external","cwd":"C:\\Users\\baenb\\projects\\MIDGE","sessionId":"141c733c-7949-435d-8de2-34bf8e29a67f","version":"2.1.71","gitBranch":"main","agentId":"a7dea850a9df9eda1","slug":"delegated-leaping-map","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01LDhbzgxc15cPG7fufAwzB5","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"geopolitical risk market signal supply chain country exposure stock impact analysis\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"Geopolitical risk and supply chain diversification | CEPR\",\"url\":\"https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/geopolitical-risk-and-supply-chain-diversification\"},{\"title\":\"Geopolitical risk and global supply chain resilience - ScienceDirect\",\"url\":\"https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/abs/pii/S1544612325018057\"},{\"title\":\"Do geopolitical risks impede the global supply chain? - ScienceDirect\",\"url\":\"https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/abs/pii/S1544612325010694\"},{\"title\":\"Geopolitical risk exposure of major stock markets hits new peak | Maplecroft\",\"url\":\"https://www.maplecroft.com/products-and-solutions/geopolitical-and-country-risk/insights/geopolitical-risk-exposure-of-major-stock-markets-hits-new-peak/\"},{\"title\":\"The rising cost of turmoil: geopolitical crises and supply chain risk - ScienceDirect\",\"url\":\"https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/abs/pii/S0165176525004197\"},{\"title\":\"Geopolitical Supply Chain Risk | Risk Ledger\",\"url\":\"https://riskledger.com/resources/supply-chains-geopolitical-de-risking\"},{\"title\":\"Frontiers | Global geopolitical risk and industrial tail risk in the Chinese stock market: a quantile-on-quantile connectedness approach\",\"url\":\"https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/physics/articles/10.3389/fphy.2025.1612695/full\"},{\"title\":\"What is the geopolitical risk and how it impacts markets | StoneX\",\"url\":\"https://www.stonex.com/en/financial-glossary/geopolitical-risk/\"},{\"title\":\"Geopolitics & Supply Chains in the Era of Exponential Risk – Moody’s\",\"url\":\"https://www.moodys.com/web/en/us/insights/exponential-risk/geopolitical-and-supply-chains.html\"},{\"title\":\"The Fed - Measuring Geopolitical Risk Exposure Across Industries: A Firm-Centered Approach\",\"url\":\"https://www.federalreserve.gov/econres/notes/feds-notes/measuring-geopolitical-risk-exposure-across-industries-a-firm-centered-approach-20250829.html\"}]\n\nBased on the web search results, I've found comprehensive information on geopolitical risk, market signals, supply chain exposure, and stock impacts. Here's an analysis of the key findings:\n\n## Geopolitical Risk and Supply Chain Dynamics\n\nGeopolitical risk significantly increases supply chain diversification, with firms exposed to rising geopolitical tensions more likely to add new import sources outside China and establish manufacturing affiliates in ASEAN countries. Firms prefer diversification because it preserves cost advantages while reducing geopolitical exposure.\n\n## Market Signals and Stock Impact\n\nStronger geopolitical risk is significantly associated with reduced supply chain resilience, as reflected in decreased volatility of financial returns. Weapon producers' volatility is predominantly driven by geopolitical acts, while they tend to lead market co-movements when shocks stem from threats alone; when geopolitical tensions escalate from threats into acts, the co-movement becomes non-directional, revealing a complex and asymmetric market behavior.\n\n## Country-Specific Exposure\n\nMany companies in technology, electronics and AI-adjacent industries, such as semiconductors, are among the most exposed to geopolitical risks, particularly in the event of a direct confrontation in Asia; for the S&P 500 these risks are potentially very material given the extremely high valuations currently associated with these industries.\n\nThe DAX is by far the most threatened, with 35% of assets at 'very high' risk on interstate tensions models measuring the risk of states engaging in disputes that include the threat, display or use of force.\n\n## Supply Chain Risk Channels\n\nGeopolitical crises significantly exacerbate supply chain risk, operating through three pathways: erosion of supply chain resilience, elevation of client concentration, and deterioration of inventory liquidity.\n\nManufacturing exhibits the highest tail risk connectedness due to its profound integration with global supply chains, and once geopolitical factors exert negative impacts on international trade via tariffs or export embargoes, manufacturing typically experiences amplified tail volatility.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"44d7e154-fade-4a70-b7d9-f4c973c6569e","timestamp":"2026-03-08T12:40:03.885Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"315f9998-0f90-468f-b38a-28fd43fe1a50"}

Wikipedia\",\"url\":\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GDELT_Project\"},{\"title\":\"GDELT Guru - AI-Powered Global Intelligence Platform\",\"url\":\"https://www.gdelt.guru/\"},{\"title\":\"Global Data on Events, Location and Tone, 1979-2012.\",\"url\":\"http://data.gdeltproject.org/documentation/ISA.2013.GDELT.pdf\"}]\n\nI've completed a web search on your query. Here are the key findings:\n\n## GDELT and CAMEO Codes Overview\n\nThe Global Database of Events, Language and Tone (GDELT) Project is a massive and extensive database of global events and related media mentions, as well as extracted/inferred information, going back to 1979. It utilizes the TABARI processing engine with the CAMEO coding system to process published media from all over the web, in almost any language, and in multiple media.\n\nThe GDELT 1.0 and 2.0 Event Databases use the CAMEO event taxonomy, which is a collection of more than 300 types of events organized into a hierarchical taxonomy and recorded in the files as a numeric code.\n\n## Financial Signals and Market Prediction\n\nResearchers have adopted GDELT for scholarly inquiries such as Media Analytics & Financial Prediction. CAMEO is a broadly used coding scheme to systematize the analysis of political and social events and divide them in a scale ranging from material and verbal cooperation to verbal and material conflict.\n\n## Geopolitical Applications\n\nEvent data provide a rich and reliable feature set to characterize geopolitical events, with CAMEO schema offering a rich array of Actor and Event codes which supports the coding of a large variety of international and domestic events. Every processed socio-political event in GDELT is coded using the Conflict and Mediation Event Observations (CAMEO) event coding system.\n\n## Data Access and Scale\n\nThe entire GDELT database is 100% free and open and you can download the raw datafiles, visualize it using the GDELT Analysis Service, or analyze it at limitless scale with Google BigQuery. The GDELT Project is the largest, most comprehensive, and highest resolution open database of human society ever created.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"4487d03e-adfa-4c56-85a1-a20cd3d182f0","timestamp":"2026-03-08T12:40:03.894Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"3af3ec62-8136-4019-9a15-f35c174b10ef"}

analysis, and financial markets as a leading indicator with temporal lag considerations:\n\n## NLP Sentiment as a Leading Indicator\n\nSentiment extracted via advanced NLP models can be an early warning indicator of financial turbulence, particularly in conflict-driven markets. Additionally, a sentiment index derived from news feeds can serve as a leading indicator of market movements, helping analysts to anticipate changes before they occur.\n\n## Real-Time Analysis and Temporal Advantages\n\nAdvances in NLP and machine learning have made real-time sentiment analysis a reality. Analysts can now process vast amounts of textual data almost instantaneously, allowing them to react swiftly to breaking news and market developments. This real-time capability gives them a significant advantage in a fast-paced trading environment, where timing is crucial.\n\n## Investor Response to Geopolitical Sentiment\n\nInvestors react more sharply to negative sentiment when uncertainty is high, mainly when such sentiment is from trusted news sources. Research also found that negative sentiment extracted from major U.S. news headlines showed a statistically significant effect on volatility, independent of economic fundamentals.\n\n## Monitoring Tools\n\nMajor institutions have developed sophisticated systems for this purpose. The BlackRock Geopolitical Risk Indicator (BGRI) tracks the relative frequency of brokerage reports (via Refinitiv) and financial news stories (Dow Jones News) associated with specific geopolitical risks.\n\nHowever, the search results don't contain specific quantitative data about temporal lags measured in days between geopolitical news sentiment signals and financial market movements.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"9aeaf4e5-b54c-41c6-9ef1-6b47bfaca127","timestamp":"2026-03-08T12:40:13.073Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"f32b4e8a-ac44-4da4-8033-d7699c386132"}

reactions to the Russia-Ukraine war and geopolitical signals:\n\n## Market Reaction Timing\n\nAn International Monetary Fund paper found that geopolitical conflicts typically cause an average one-month equity market decline of around 1%, suggesting markets adjust fairly quickly to assess geopolitical risks.\n\nThe stock and foreign exchange market initially responded swiftly, serving as starting points for risk diffusion, indicating these markets are among the first to react to geopolitical signals.\n\n## Oil and Gas Market Sensitivity\n\nOil markets appear to be the most sensitive because conflict can change expectations about supply, shipping routes, and energy infrastructure. American Economic Association\",\"url\":\"https://www.aeaweb.org/articles?id=10.1257/aer.20191823\"},{\"title\":\"K.7 Measuring Geopolitical Risk Caldara, Dario and Matteo Iacoviello\",\"url\":\"https://www.federalreserve.gov/econres/ifdp/files/ifdp1222.pdf\"},{\"title\":\"Measuring Geopolitical Risk\",\"url\":\"https://www.matteoiacoviello.com/gpr_files/GPR_PAPER.pdf\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's what I found about the Geopolitical Risk Index (GPR) by Caldara and Iacoviello:\n\n## Overview\n\nDario Caldara and Matteo Iacoviello construct a new measure of adverse geopolitical events based on a tally of newspaper articles covering geopolitical tensions, and examine its evolution and economic effects since 1900. The index measures the occurrence of impactful geopolitical events/threats/conflicts since 1985 by counting the keywords used in the press.\n\n## Data Availability and Free Download\n\nThe data is available free from multiple sources:\n\n1. **Official Webpage**: Visit the Geopolitical Risk Index Webpage for additional details and data. The main source is at https://www.matteoiacoviello.com/gpr.htm\n\n2. **Academic Archive**: The archive contains files to reproduce the results in \"Measuring Geopolitical Risk\" as well as any additional documentation. Updated data can be found on the geopolitical risk index webpage at https://www.matteoiacoviello.com/gpr.htm\n\n## Index Components\n\nThe Benchmark Index (GPR) uses 10 newspapers and starts in 1985. Caldara and Iacoviello use the same methodology to construct a Geopolitical Risk Historical Index (GPRH), which uses three newspapers and starts in 1900.\n\n## Market Signal and Economic Impact\n\nHigher geopolitical risk foreshadows lower investment and employment and is associated with higher disaster probability and larger downside risks. They complement aggregate measures with industry- and firm-level indicators of geopolitical risk. Investment drops more in industries that are exposed to aggregate geopolitical risk.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"8ece93da-edbb-4c70-ae8d-f4fbdb0f2ff4","timestamp":"2026-03-08T12:40:25.303Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"95aa9d19-6979-418c-af2d-ca66a75206d9"}

Internally it uses regular expressions to match country names.\n\n**Supported Classification Schemes:**\n\nThe library supports ISO2 (ISO 3166-1 alpha-2) codes, including non-standard codes for disputed or dissolved countries. Additional classification schemes include GWcode (Gledisch & Ward numerical codes), CC41 (common classification for MRIOs), IOC (International Olympic Committee) country codes, and BACI (International Trade Database at the Product-Level).\n\n**Advanced Functionality:**\n\nCoco can also be used to build aggregation concordance matrices between different classification schemes. In addition, coco includes classification based on UN-, EU-, OECD-membership, UN regions specifications, continents and various MRIO and IAM databases.\n\n**Related Libraries:**\n\nThe search results also highlight other Python libraries for country code handling:\n- pycountry is a Python library to access ISO country, subdivision, language, currency and script definitions and their translations.\n- The HDX Python Country Library provides utilities to map between country and region codes and names and to match administrative level names from different sources.\n\n**Note on Supply Chain and Sector Exposure:** The search results do not specifically address supply chain sector exposure mapping functionality. linwoodc3/gdeltPyR: Python based framework to retreive Global Database of Events, Language, and Tone (GDELT) version 1.0 and version 2.0 data.\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/linwoodc3/gdeltPyR\"},{\"title\":\"Global Database of Events, Language and Tone (GDELT) ...\",\"url\":\"https://backup.ons.gov.uk/wp-content/uploads/sites/3/2020/01/Global-Database-of-Events-Language-and-Tone-GDELT-appendix.pdf\"},{\"title\":\"Gdelt Dataset: Events, Mentions, and Global Knowledge Graph — 计算传播学\",\"url\":\"https://chengjun.github.io/mybook/03-gdelt.html\"},{\"title\":\"The GDELT Project\",\"url\":\"https://www.gdeltproject.org/\"}]\n\nI've completed the web search for your query about GDELT GKG global knowledge graph tone goldstein scale Python financial market prediction. Here are the key findings:\n\n## Overview of GDELT and GKG\n\nThe Global Data on Events, Location, and Tone (GDELT) is a real time large scale database of global human society for open research which monitors world's broadcast, print, and web news, creating a free open platform for computing on the entire world's media.\n\nGDELT consists of two main datasets, the \"Global Knowledge Graph (GKG)\" and the \"Events Table\". GDELT's GKG captures what's happening around the world, what its context is, who's involved, and how the world is feeling about it; every single day.\n\n## Tone and Emotional Measures\n\nThe Global Content Analysis Measures (GCAM) system runs each document through an array of content analysis systems to assess several thousand latent dimensions for each article. GDELT measures thousands of emotional dimensions expressed by means of popular dictionaries in the literature, such as the \"Harvard IV-4 Psychosocial Dictionary\", the \"WordNet-Affect dictionary\", and the \"Loughran and McDonald Sentiment Word Lists dictionary\", among others.\n\n## Goldstein Scale\n\nThe Goldstein scale depends on the CAMEO Event type and does not consider any further context.\n\n## Python Tools\n\nUnlike the primary GDELT event stream, the GKG file format requires more sophisticated preprocessing and users will likely want to make use of a scripting language like PERL or Python to extract and reprocess the data for import into a statistical package. gdeltPyR is a Python based framework to retrieve GDELT version 1.0 and version 2.0 data.\n\n## Financial Market Prediction Applications\n\nBy exploiting and engineering the detailed information of each news encoded in GDELT, researchers build indicators capturing investor's emotions which are useful to analyse the sovereign bond market, and using regression analysis and Gradient Boosting models from machine learning, features extracted from GDELT improve the forecast of country government yield spread.\n\nFive sentiment measures representing the news media Tone, Optimism, Attention, Tone Dispersion, and Emotional Polarity of Chinese stock markets are constructed based on article tone scores and media coverages from GDELT, and all these news media sentiment measures are shown to have significant predictive power for Chinese stock market returns and volatilities.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"90a5a70e-cc81-4101-9a5d-acf3bbd36be3","timestamp":"2026-03-08T12:40:28.229Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"04eec7fb-5794-46cb-bebe-28329cc00f24"}

One notable example is the jeffallen13/ofac repository, where monthly-download.py is the main script for updating the OFAC data and saving the datasets to the data/ folder.\n\nThe lists branch of this repository contains automatically updated lists of sanctioned addresses for each covered asset, generated each night at 0 UTC by a GitHub Actions workflow. This demonstrates using git branches to track changes in OFAC-related data.\n\n## Extracting and Processing Data\n\nFor working with OFAC data programmatically, the SDN list as XML file can be downloaded via wget from the Treasury website. By default, the python script extracts addresses into a TXT file.\n\n## Monitoring and Compliance\n\nOFAC has an email subscription service that will notify when there are updates to the sanctions list. Additionally, Moov Watchman is a high-performance sanctions screening and compliance tool that provides an HTTP server and Go library for searching against multiple global sanctions and screening lists.\n\nFor Python-based monitoring solutions, there are several projects on GitHub focused on scraping and tracking OFAC sanctions data with version control integration available through public repositories.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"cdbf30e9-0c6b-49b7-8070-239fc3a571da","timestamp":"2026-03-08T12:40:38.365Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"2fbd54b3-df99-4b4b-ad79-4f859e84bbb7"}

ScienceDirect\",\"url\":\"https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/abs/pii/S0308596125000485\"},{\"title\":\"Geopolitics of Semiconductor Supply Chains: The Case of TSMC, US-China-Taiwan Relations, and the COVID-19 Crisis\",\"url\":\"https://digitalcollections.sit.edu/isp_collection/3592/\"},{\"title\":\"Taiwan Semiconductor Puts Global Tech Supply Chains Under Geopolitical Scrutiny - Research updates | Efficio Consulting\",\"url\":\"https://www.efficioconsulting.com/en-us/resources/all/china-taiwan-tensions-impacts-on-global-supply-chains-and-semiconductor-availability/\"},{\"title\":\"(Analysis) Chips and Power: How TSMC Navigates Geopolitical Tensions - The New Global Order\",\"url\":\"https://thenewglobalorder.com/world-news/analysis-chips-and-power-how-tsmc-navigates-geopolitical-tensions/\"},{\"title\":\"Silicon Island: Assessing Taiwan’s Importance to U.S. Economic Growth and Security | CSIS\",\"url\":\"https://www.csis.org/analysis/silicon-island-assessing-taiwans-importance-us-economic-growth-and-security\"},{\"title\":\"The Taiwan Semiconductor Risk: The $10 Trillion Chokepoint\",\"url\":\"https://longyield.substack.com/p/the-taiwan-semiconductor-risk-the\"},{\"title\":\"How Taiwan’s Chip Industry Navigates US Industrial Policy and Export Controls | Global Taiwan Institute\",\"url\":\"https://globaltaiwan.org/2025/03/how-taiwans-chip-industry-navigates-us-industrial-policy-and-export-controls/\"}]\n\nBased on the web search results, here's what I found on Taiwan semiconductor supply chain stocks, TSMC, geopolitical tensions, and market impact:\n\n## Taiwan's Semiconductor Dominance & Market Concentration\n\nTSMC has reached roughly 70% global foundry share with a market value of around US$2 trillion, making it the world's dominant chipmaker. TSMC's global market share in dedicated contract chipmaking rose to 64% in 2024, up from 60% the year prior, driven by surging demand for chips powering artificial intelligence, smartphones, and advanced computing systems.\n\n## Concentration Risk & Geopolitical Vulnerability\n\nThe critical vulnerability lies in extreme concentration. TSMC produces ~92% of the world's most advanced semiconductors (<5nm), and no other company can substitute this capacity within 3–5 years. Every major AI accelerator chip — Nvidia's H100/B100, AMD's MI300X, Google's TPU, Amazon's Trainium, Broadcom's custom ASICs — is manufactured and packaged exclusively by TSMC with no second source, making a TSMC disruption functionally an AI compute disruption.\n\n## Potential Economic Impact\n\nThe downside risk is substantial. IEP estimates that a full-scale conflict over Taiwan could result in a $10 trillion loss to the global economy. Global GDP impacts range from 0.5–1.5% in the mildest disruption case to 5–10% in a sustained disruption.\n\n## Market Response & Second-Order Effects\n\nKey customers such as Nvidia, Apple and AMD's choices about second sourcing and inventory strategies help shape how much Taiwan specific risk remains in their supply chains. Global semiconductor lead times have normalized to ~14 weeks, but most end-users hold only 4–8 weeks of chip inventory, creating limited buffers against disruptions.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"d427e2fd-1d28-40f0-925a-487433e6f827","timestamp":"2026-03-08T12:40:41.497Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"4cbce3e0-ddba-4afe-814d-d247b6ae0a0b"}

Libraries.io - security & maintenance data for open source software\",\"url\":\"https://libraries.io/pypi/opensanctions\"},{\"title\":\"OpenSanctions | Bellingcat's Online Investigation Toolkit\",\"url\":\"https://bellingcat.gitbook.io/toolkit/more/all-tools/opensanctions\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's what I found about OpenSanctions API:\n\n## Overview\n\nOpenSanctions aggregates and provides a comprehensive open-source database of sanctions data, politically exposed persons, and related entities.\n\n## API Capabilities\n\nThe API lets you easily integrate OpenSanctions into your workflow, allowing you to search the database and conduct batch screening to identify people or companies that are included on a sanctions list or linked to politically exposed persons (PEPs).\n\n## Python Integration\n\nThe OpenSanctions codebase is an open-source repository of sanctions data, politically exposed persons, and other entities of interest, and uses Follow the Money, a JSON-based anti-corruption data model, as a common target for all crawlers. The package is available on PyPI for Python integration.\n\n## Free Access & Licensing\n\nOpenSanctions is free for non-commercial users, though businesses must acquire a data license to use the dataset. The code is MIT-licensed, and content & data is CC 4.0 Attribution-NonCommercial.\n\n## Data Coverage\n\nOpenSanctions has 269 separate dataset collections with multiple sources of data, and features consolidated sanctions on 75,746 entities.\n\nThe API documentation is available at https://www.opensanctions.org/docs/api/ for detailed integration guidance.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"a7342044-7a31-49c3-b397-4e541fb0309d","timestamp":"2026-03-08T12:40:49.842Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"6685e7e6-03c9-4b4d-bb0b-e070618e30a2"}

The API allows you to return articles that cover one of GDELT's GKG Themes.\n\n## 15-Minute Update Rate\n\nFor time spans of less than 72 hours, the timeline uses a time step of 15 minutes to provide maximum temporal resolution, while for time spans from 72 hours to one week it uses an hourly resolution and for time spans of greater than a week it uses a daily resolution.\n\n## Free Access\n\nThe search results indicate that the GDELT DOC API is freely available and accessible. ScienceDirect\",\"url\":\"https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/abs/pii/S0165176525004197\"},{\"title\":\"The oil price surge is just one symptom of a supply chain network that is not fit for this age of global tensions\",\"url\":\"https://theconversation.com/the-oil-price-surge-is-just-one-symptom-of-a-supply-chain-network-that-is-not-fit-for-this-age-of-global-tensions-277277\"},{\"title\":\"Geopolitical disruptions in global supply chains: The role of strategic alliances - Here's what I found about geopolitical events, market reactions, and second-order supply chain cascading effects:\n\n## Key Research Findings\n\n**Market Reactions and Cascading Effects:**\n\nGeopolitical events cause delays and costs that cascade through production supply chains, corporate bottom lines, and customer sales, creating ripples in the macroeconomy. Disruptions at critical nodes trigger cascading effects across global systems, and disruptions in one region can quickly spread to others in a domino effect.\n\n**Research on Market Reaction Mechanisms:**\n\nScholars predominantly employ the geopolitical risk (GPR) index to quantify crises—a metric tracking media frequency of keywords related to warfare, terrorism, and interstate confrontations. Caldara and Iacoviello (2022) develop a widely used Geopolitical Risk (GPR) index based on newspaper coverage of geopolitical tensions, showing that geopolitical shocks have measurable macroeconomic and financial effects.\n\n**Second-Order Effects and Supply Chain Dynamics:**\n\nGeopolitical conflict risk exhibits multi-phase nature: short-term panic-driven supply–demand shocks, medium-term supply chain restructuring, and long-term policy feedback. The lagged correlation test results show that the correlation coefficient between shipping indices and geopolitical risk declines significantly at a lag of approximately one month, which may stem from delays in policy interventions or supply chain restructuring.\n\n**Methodology Gaps:**\n\nWhile existing literature encompasses various methodological approaches from game-theoretic models to mathematical optimization techniques, most contributions predominantly focus on reactive strategies, with comparatively few studies advancing proactive planning frameworks, revealing a notable methodological gap.\n\nThe research indicates that understanding second-order cascading effects requires integrated analysis of both immediate market shocks and the delayed adaptation of supply chains over weeks to months.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"cb918516-1cdc-4cd4-8956-a77cc63f0205","timestamp":"2026-03-08T12:40:53.003Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"ed862a1d-63c8-4207-99aa-c64c7a3d0119"}

{"parentUuid":"5e7b7eb2-87e3-4178-9422-fc795a6dd7f3","isSidechain":true,"userType":"external","cwd":"C:\\Users\\baenb\\projects\\MIDGE","sessionId":"141c733c-7949-435d-8de2-34bf8e29a67f","version":"2.1.71","gitBranch":"main","agentId":"a7dea850a9df9eda1","slug":"delegated-leaping-map","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01XfZHf9TcgugXALEgp2RhYG","type":"tool_result","content":"# GDELT Data Feeds for Financial Market Signal Detection\n\n## Main Data Feeds & Update Frequencies\n\n**GDELT 2.0 Event Database**\n- Updates: Every 15 minutes\n- Format: CSV (downloadable) or Google BigQuery\n- Coverage: 65 live translated languages\n- Includes new \"mentions\" table with additional fields\n\n**GDELT 2.0 Global Knowledge Graph (GKG)**\n- Updates: Every 15 minutes\n- Format: CSV or BigQuery\n- Features: Connects \"people, organizations, locations, themes, counts, images and emotions\"\n\n**GDELT APIs (Real-time JSON)**\n- DOC API: Fulltext search capabilities\n- GEO API: Location-based data from 1.6+ billion mentions\n- TV API: Television news analysis\n\n## Fields Relevant to Market Signals\n\n**Event Data (CAMEO Taxonomy):**\n- Event codes (300+ types, hierarchical)\n- Actor codes (countries, organizations, groups)\n- Goldstein Scale (sentiment scoring for events)\n- Geographic references (locations, coordinates)\n\n**Knowledge Graph Data:**\n- Entity mentions with context\n- Sentiment/emotional scores\n- Counts (protests, casualties, affected populations)\n- Themes and topics\n\n## Access Methods\n\n1. **GDELT Analysis Service** – Free, browser-based visualization (limited to GDELT 1.0)\n2. **Google BigQuery** – \"Near-realtime\" complex SQL queries across full datasets\n3. **Raw CSV Downloads** – Direct file access (2.5TB+ annually)\n\n**For market applications:** BigQuery enables rapid scanning for conflict events, policy changes, and humanitarian crises affecting specific regions or commodities."}]},"uuid":"7a35a385-b487-4eb5-b041-81b8661ec02c","timestamp":"2026-03-08T12:41:02.465Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"5e7b7eb2-87e3-4178-9422-fc795a6dd7f3"}

{"parentUuid":"a8b96bc1-36b2-4262-b6f8-d1abb5bf6e49","isSidechain":true,"userType":"external","cwd":"C:\\Users\\baenb\\projects\\MIDGE","sessionId":"141c733c-7949-435d-8de2-34bf8e29a67f","version":"2.1.71","gitBranch":"main","agentId":"a7dea850a9df9eda1","slug":"delegated-leaping-map","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01WeJTdoqUTJsuA5EQgk7KZG","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"SEC EDGAR 6-K foreign private issuer geopolitical event filing market signal\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"SEC.gov | Mandated EDGAR Filing For Foreign Issuers\",\"url\":\"https://www.sec.gov/rules/2002/05/mandated-edgar-filing-foreign-issuers\"},{\"title\":\"SEC.gov | EDGAR Full Text Search\",\"url\":\"https://www.sec.gov/edgar/search/\"},{\"title\":\"SEC Mandates EDGAR Filing for Foreign Issuers | News & Resources | Dorsey\",\"url\":\"https://www.dorsey.com/newsresources/publications/2002/06/sec-mandates-edgar-filing-for-foreign-issuers\"},{\"title\":\"SEC EDGAR Filing Service | Foreign Issuer forms F1 F3 20F 6K 6F S8\",\"url\":\"https://edgarfiling.acii.com/foreign.htm\"},{\"title\":\"Form 6-K\",\"url\":\"https://www.sec.gov/files/form6-k.pdf\"},{\"title\":\"SEC.gov | Search Filings\",\"url\":\"https://www.sec.gov/search-filings\"},{\"title\":\"satl20240522_6k.htm\",\"url\":\"https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1874315/000143774924018333/satl20240522_6k.htm\"},{\"title\":\"The Secrets of Form 6-K - Words Of Wisdom\",\"url\":\"https://www.wowlw.com/Article/Index/284%20?refertype=Topic&refname=Client+Alerts\"},{\"title\":\"SEC.gov | Statement on Concept Release on Foreign Private Issuer Eligibility\",\"url\":\"https://www.sec.gov/newsroom/speeches-statements/atkins-foreign-private-issuer-060425\"},{\"title\":\"SEC.gov | Financial Reporting Manual\",\"url\":\"https://www.sec.gov/about/divisions-offices/division-corporation-finance/financial-reporting-manual/frm-topic-6\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's information about SEC EDGAR Form 6-K filings for foreign private issuers:\n\n## Form 6-K Overview\n\nForm 6-K is used by foreign private issuers which are required to furnish reports pursuant to Rule 13a-16 or 15d-16 under the Securities Exchange Act of 1934. This form is used to report specific events or information that the issuer is required to disclose under the securities laws of its home country. These reports provide updates on material events, financial results, and other important information.\n\n## What Must Be Disclosed\n\nAn issuer furnishing a report on this form shall furnish whatever information that the issuer (i) makes or is required to make public pursuant to the law of the jurisdiction of its domicile or in which it is incorporated or organized, or (ii) files or is required to file with a stock exchange on which its securities are traded and which was made public by that exchange, or (iii) distributes or is required to distribute to its security holders.\n\n## Filing Requirements\n\nAn issuer must submit the Form 6-K report in electronic format via the Commission's Electronic Data Gathering, Analysis, and Retrieval (EDGAR) system in accordance with the EDGAR rules set forth in Regulation S-T.\n\n## Key Distinction from Domestic Filers\n\nForeign private issuers furnish current reports on Form 6-K, rather than filing the more prescriptive Form 8-K, which gives them more flexibility compared to U.S. domestic companies.\n\nRegarding geopolitical events specifically, the search results do not contain detailed information about how geopolitical events are disclosed in 6-K filings or their market signaling implications. You may want to consult the actual Form 6-K documents on EDGAR to see examples of how specific companies disclose geopolitical risks or events.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"f57df2ac-e697-4472-bcf7-adb81914b3a1","timestamp":"2026-03-08T12:41:03.731Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"a8b96bc1-36b2-4262-b6f8-d1abb5bf6e49"}

Index\",\"url\":\"https://www.matteoiacoviello.com/gpr_country.htm\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, I found information about the Geopolitical Risk Index by Caldara and Iacoviello:\n\n## Overview\n\nThe Geopolitical Risk Index (GPR) is compiled by Fed economists Dario Caldara and Matteo Iacoviello and measures the occurrence of impactful geopolitical events/threats/conflicts since 1985 by counting keywords used in the press.\n\n## Data Sources and Structure\n\nThe GPR index is based on automated text-search results from 10 newspapers (Chicago Tribune, Daily Telegraph, Financial Times, The Globe and Mail, The Guardian, Los Angeles Times, The New York Times, USA Today, The Wall Street Journal, and The Washington Post), calculated by counting the number of articles related to adverse geopolitical events in each newspaper for each month.\n\n## Accessing the Data\n\nThe primary resources for accessing monthly GPR data are:\n\n1. **Official Geopolitical Risk Index Webpage**: The main data repository is located at https://www.matteoiacoviello.com/gpr.htm\n\n2. **Replication Materials**: Monthly Geopolitical Risk Data is available in both Stata format (data_gpr_export.dta) and Excel format (data_gpr_export.xls), with materials available on the replication page at https://www.matteoiacoviello.com/gpr_replication.htm\n\nRegarding FRED availability: While the search results don't explicitly confirm a CSV download option directly from FRED, the official sources above provide the data in multiple formats that can be used for analysis.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown Here's what I found:\n\n## gdeltdoc Python Library Overview\n\nThe gdeltdoc is a client for the GDELT 2.0 Doc API. Filters can be configured with parameters including start_date, end_date, num_records, keyword, domain, country, language and theme.\n\n## CrisisLex Themes\n\nTwo CrisisLex taxonomies, \"CrisisLexRec – Recommended CrisisLex Lexicon\" and \"EMTerms – Terminological Resource for Crisis Tweets\" are now available within the GDELT GKG and are prefaced with \"CRISISLEX_\". \n\nThe search results mention `CRISISLEX_CRISISLEXREC` as one of the available theme options. These lexicons were built specifically for use with social media (Twitter in particular) and thus do not map perfectly onto the language used in traditional mainstream media.\n\nRegarding the specific theme codes you mentioned (such as `CRISISLEX_CRISISMENTION`, `sanctions`, `military conflict`), the search results show that themes can be passed as filter parameters, but they don't provide a complete list of all available theme codes including those specific terms. The results demonstrate example usage with `\"GENERAL_HEALTH\"` as a theme, but the detailed code list for crisis-related themes isn't fully detailed in these results.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"65df2b6d-e94d-4031-8095-02510b70aa7f","timestamp":"2026-03-08T12:41:20.049Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"88a3df6b-8539-4ae1-a97c-cb1f63359aee"}

Office for National Statistics\",\"url\":\"https://www.ons.gov.uk/peoplepopulationandcommunity/birthsdeathsandmarriages/deaths/methodologies/globaldatabaseofeventslanguageandtonegdeltappendix\"},{\"title\":\"THE GDELT EVENT DATABASE DATA FORMAT CODEBOOK V2.0 2/19/2015\",\"url\":\"http://data.gdeltproject.org/documentation/GDELT-Event_Codebook-V2.0.pdf\"},{\"title\":\"Over 100 New GKG Themes Added – The GDELT Project\",\"url\":\"https://blog.gdeltproject.org/over-100-new-gkg-themes-added/\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's what I found about GDELT GKG theme codes:\n\n## GDELT GKG Theme Codes Overview\n\nThere are thousands of different GKG themes, with the list currently including over 2,500 different themes.\n\n### Resources for Complete Lists\n\nA CSV file of all known GDELT Global Knowledge Graph (GKG) Themes is available on GitHub. Additionally, a comprehensive list can be found at: http://data.gdeltproject.org/documentation/GDELT-Global_Knowledge_Graph_CategoryList.xlsx\n\nThe GDELT Project has published an updated GKG Themes lookup file called LOOKUP-GKGTHEMES.TXT available for download.\n\n### Theme Categories\n\nGKG themes range from economic indicators like price gouging and the price of heating oil to infrastructure topics like the construction of new power generation capacity to social issues like marginalization and burning in effigy. \n\nThe GDELT Global Content Analysis Measures (GCAM) module assesses more than 2,300 emotions and themes from every article in realtime.\n\nGDELT has also added over 600 global humanitarian and development aid organizations, global currencies, and a massively expanded list of global ethnic groups and languages.\n\nFor the specific military, sanctions, trade war, and geopolitical category codes you're looking for, you would need to consult the actual category list files linked above, as the search results don't provide the specific codes for those categories.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"3478d9c9-8d1a-42c4-b1b5-f73fd32e7f62","timestamp":"2026-03-08T12:41:33.788Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"609570a5-a60b-45ea-a88c-06e686d59ec0"}

benryan58/sipri_arms: Unofficial pseudo-API wrapper for downloading SIPR Arms Transfer Database information, and some tools for manipulating and visualizing the data.\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/benryan58/sipri_arms\"},{\"title\":\"Arms transfer database\",\"url\":\"https://armstransfers.sipri.org/ArmsTransfer/TransferData\"},{\"title\":\"SIPRI Arms Transfers Database | SIPRI\",\"url\":\"https://www.sipri.org/databases/armstransfers\"},{\"title\":\"How to download SIPRI arms transfer data as a CSV file instead of a rich-text file. · GitHub\",\"url\":\"https://gist.github.com/jsvine/9cb3300588ed402160fe\"},{\"title\":\"SIPRI databases | SIPRI\",\"url\":\"https://www.sipri.org/databases\"},{\"title\":\"BW #57: International arms trade (solution)\",\"url\":\"https://www.bambooweekly.com/bw-57-international-arms-trade-solution/\"},{\"title\":\"SIPRI Arms Transfers Database\",\"url\":\"https://armstransfers.sipri.org/\"},{\"title\":\"Arms Transfers Database - knoema.com\",\"url\":\"https://knoema.com/SIPRIATD2018/arms-transfers-database\"},{\"title\":\"Measuring international arms transfers\",\"url\":\"https://www.sipri.org/sites/default/files/files/FS/SIPRIFS1212.pdf\"},{\"title\":\"Sources and methods | SIPRI\",\"url\":\"https://www.sipri.org/databases/armstransfers/sources-and-methods\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's what I found regarding your query about SIPRI arms transfers database with API and Python access:\n\n## Key Resources\n\n**SIPRI Arms Transfers Database**\nThe SIPRI Arms Transfers Database contains information on all transfers of major conventional arms from 1950 to the most recent full calendar year. The publicly accessible database is updated annually.\n\n**Python API Wrapper**\nThere is an unofficial pseudo-API wrapper created for the SIPRI Arms Transfers Database web interface, available on GitHub at `benryan58/sipri_arms`. The wrapper is for downloading SIPR Arms Transfer Database information, and includes some tools for manipulating and visualizing the data.\n\nThere are two potential endpoints for queries: one for the \"Trade Registers\" with detailed arms trade listings, and one for \"trend-indicator value\" (TIV) listings, with the latter summarizing arms transfers by value. By default, data is returned as a Pandas DataFrame object in CSV format, but can be changed to any of RTF, HTML, or JSON formats.\n\n**Military Spending Data**\nThe SIPRI Military Expenditure Database gives the annual military spending of countries since 1949, allowing comparison of countries' military spending in local currency at current prices; in US dollars at constant prices and exchange rates; and as a share of GDP.\n\n**Important Notes**\nThe SIPRI TIV is often misinterpreted as a financial value, but it neither reflects the actual price paid for weapons nor represents current dollar values for arms transfers.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"33fc5c60-f85d-4116-9fec-665f13c7d98b","timestamp":"2026-03-08T12:41:35.862Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"843c7d80-2d18-4b33-a073-acf36a6671b6"}

{"parentUuid":"4a511d02-0912-42fb-bc06-7fbf84dd2528","isSidechain":true,"userType":"external","cwd":"C:\\Users\\baenb\\projects\\MIDGE","sessionId":"141c733c-7949-435d-8de2-34bf8e29a67f","version":"2.1.71","gitBranch":"main","agentId":"a7dea850a9df9eda1","slug":"delegated-leaping-map","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_014AywGBRRJWeugWUB96n6ag","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"geopolitical temporal lead market reaction energy stocks semiconductors shipping timing window study\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"Semiconductor game of thrones: A comprehensive study of geopolitical and equity market uncertainty transmission - ScienceDirect\",\"url\":\"https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S1057521925005447\"},{\"title\":\"Frontiers | Research on the volatility spillover effects of geopolitical conflict risks on international shipping and crude oil markets\",\"url\":\"https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/marine-science/articles/10.3389/fmars.2025.1647599/full\"},{\"title\":\"This Week's Market Wrap: Energy, Defense Stocks Take the Lead as Oil Prices Spike Higher | Financial Sense\",\"url\":\"https://www.financialsense.com/blog/21578/weeks-market-wrap-energy-defense-stocks-take-lead-oil-prices-spike-higher\"},{\"title\":\"Temporal dynamics of geopolitical risk: An empirical study on energy commodity interest-adjusted spreads - ScienceDirect\",\"url\":\"https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/abs/pii/S0140988324007758\"},{\"title\":\"Geopolitical Shock vs. Corporate Fundamentals: A Macro Analysis of Select Stock Reactions\",\"url\":\"https://www.ainvest.com/news/geopolitical-shock-corporate-fundamentals-macro-analysis-select-stock-reactions-2603/\"},{\"title\":\"Top geopolitical risks 2025: Energy insights\",\"url\":\"https://kpmg.com/xx/en/our-insights/risk-and-regulation/top-risks-forecast/energy.html\"},{\"title\":\"Geopolitical Conflict and Impact on Global Markets | U.S. Bank\",\"url\":\"https://www.usbank.com/investing/financial-perspectives/market-news/russia-ukraine-global-market.html\"},{\"title\":\"Building resilient semiconductor supply chains amid global tensions Omdia\",\"url\":\"https://omdia.tech.informa.com/blogs/2025/sep/building-resilient-semiconductor-supply-chains-amid-global-tensions\"},{\"title\":\"The risk spillover between geopolitical risk and China's 5G, semiconductor and rare earth industries - ScienceDirect\",\"url\":\"https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S2405844024160793\"},{\"title\":\"Full article: Stock-market responses, oil-price dynamics, and geopolitical risk in the MEA region\",\"url\":\"https://www.tandfonline.com/doi/full/10.1080/00036846.2025.2545019\"}]\n\nI've found several relevant research studies on how geopolitical events affect market reactions across energy, semiconductors, and shipping sectors. Here's what the search results reveal:\n\n## Key Research Findings\n\n**Temporal Dynamics and Market Reactions:**\n\nAn International Monetary Fund paper found that geopolitical events typically cause an average one-month equity market decline of around 1%, which helps explain why a sharp first reaction may fade. Additionally, some markets exhibit a significant long-run relationship with oil prices and geopolitical risk while showing no short-run effects, suggesting that financial markets require time to absorb and respond to external shocks through gradual transmission of oil price fluctuations into macroeconomic fundamentals.\n\n**Sector-Specific Responses:**\n\nThe interest-adjusted spread (IAS) of WTI crude oil is more sensitive to geopolitical risk compared to natural gas, with the effect of geopolitical risk diminishing over long-term maturities. For semiconductors, there exists risk spillover between geopolitical risk and China's 5G, semiconductor, and rare earth industries, which is significantly enhanced under the impact of geopolitical events.\n\n**Shipping and Oil Market Connections:**\n\nThe impact of geopolitical shocks on the oil market is event-driven. Research also shows that policy formulation and implementation typically require time to assess and address complex situations, with approvals for rerouting permits involving coordination across multiple agencies.\n\nThe search results include peer-reviewed studies examining temporal lead indicators, but the most comprehensive research appears focused on spillover effects and asymmetric market responses rather than specific \"temporal lead\" windows as framed in your query.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"69414800-4fe7-469f-9f3c-0e96b1ec62b7","timestamp":"2026-03-08T12:41:45.532Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"4a511d02-0912-42fb-bc06-7fbf84dd2528"}

Morningstar Global Geographic Segment Methodology\",\"url\":\"https://indexes.morningstar.com/api/docs/685c251a8b2a6a05a909c77b\"},{\"title\":\"SEC.gov | EDGAR Application Programming Interfaces (APIs)\",\"url\":\"https://www.sec.gov/search-filings/edgar-application-programming-interfaces\"},{\"title\":\"SEC EDGAR Filings API\",\"url\":\"https://sec-api.io/\"},{\"title\":\"Extract Google's Revenue Metrics from 10-K Filings with Python\",\"url\":\"https://sec-api.io/resources/extract-googles-revenue-metrics-from-10-k-filings-with-python\"},{\"title\":\"Extract Financial Statements from SEC Filings with Python\",\"url\":\"https://sec-api.io/resources/extract-financial-statements-from-sec-filings-and-xbrl-data-with-python\"},{\"title\":\"data.sec.gov\",\"url\":\"https://data.sec.gov/\"},{\"title\":\"sec-api · PyPI\",\"url\":\"https://pypi.org/project/sec-api/\"},{\"title\":\"GitHub - janlukasschroeder/sec-api-python: Python SEC EDGAR Filings API. Over 18 million filings, all 150 filing types supported. Query, full-text search and real-time stream API. Convert XBRL-to-JSON and access standardized financial statements from 10-K and 10-Q filings.\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/janlukasschroeder/sec-api-python\"},{\"title\":\"EDGAR XBRL Guide Prepared by SEC Staff February 2026\",\"url\":\"https://www.sec.gov/files/edgar/filer-information/specifications/xbrl-guide.pdf\"},{\"title\":\"FactSet GeoRev API\",\"url\":\"https://developer.factset.com/api-catalog/factset-georev-api\"}]\n\nI found comprehensive information about company country exposure, SEC 10-K geographic revenue segment data, and available APIs and databases. Here's a summary of the key resources:\n\n## SEC APIs for 10-K Geographic Revenue Data\n\nThe SEC's data.sec.gov hosts RESTful data APIs delivering JSON-formatted data, including XBRL data from financial statements such as 10-K, 10-Q, 8-K forms. These APIs do not require any authentication or API keys to access.\n\n## Third-Party API Solutions\n\nSeveral specialized APIs provide structured access to geographic revenue segment data:\n\n1. **sec-api.io**: A SEC EDGAR filings API offering query access to historical filings, live feed streaming, filing mapping to ticker/CIK/SIC codes, support for over 150 filing types from 1993 to present in JSON format.\n\n2. **XBRL-to-JSON Conversion**: Using the XBRL-to-JSON Converter API, revenue metrics can be extracted from XBRL data of 10-K filings and converted into standardized JSON format.\n\n## Geographic Segment Data Processing\n\nMorningstar provides country-level geographic segment data that can be used to calculate a company's or portfolio's economic exposure to different geographic regions. Morningstar assigns all values down to the individual country level and can provide this information to downstream products for aggregation.\n\n## XBRL Dimension Structure\n\nA taxonomy might define a \"Country\" axis with explicit members representing countries, allowing distinct facts such as revenue by country to be tracked.\n\nThese resources provide comprehensive access to company country exposure and geographic revenue segment data from SEC 10-K filings through multiple APIs and databases.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"d99fd04b-d89c-41aa-aab2-96c870fa588e","timestamp":"2026-03-08T12:41:49.115Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"0d5232d0-d6fc-4d56-9fba-412749a942b2"}

ScienceDirect\",\"url\":\"https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S1057521925005447\"},{\"title\":\"FinancialContent - S&P 500 Technical Breakdown: Geopolitical Risk Pushes Index Below Key Support\",\"url\":\"https://markets.financialcontent.com/stocks/article/marketminute-2026-3-5-s-and-p-500-technical-breakdown-geopolitical-risk-pushes-index-below-key-support\"},{\"title\":\"How Geopolitical Tensions Drive U.S. Stock Market Volatility in 2026 | by Ajayprhrrr | Feb, 2026 | Medium\",\"url\":\"https://medium.com/@ajayprhrrr0321/how-geopolitical-tensions-drive-u-s-stock-market-volatility-in-2026-b5ec95226ebf\"},{\"title\":\"Impact of geopolitical risks and innovation on global defense stock return | PLOS One\",\"url\":\"https://journals.plos.org/plosone/article?id=10.1371/journal.pone.0312155\"},{\"title\":\"Geopolitical Risk and Stock Prices\",\"url\":\"https://economics.fiu.edu/research/working-papers/2024/2407.pdf\"},{\"title\":\"Impact of geopolitical risks and innovation on global defense stock return - PMC\",\"url\":\"https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12404389/\"},{\"title\":\"What's really got the U.S. stock market spooked about this latest geopolitical conflict\",\"url\":\"https://www.cnbc.com/2026/03/03/whats-really-got-the-us-stock-market-worried-about-this-latest-war.html\"}]\n\nBased on the web search results, here's what recent research shows about geopolitical event stock market reactions across energy, defense, and semiconductor sectors:\n\n## Defense Sector\n\n50.6% of defense companies experienced immediate effects following the Crimean Peninsula's annexation, and the Russia–Ukraine war impacted the stocks of 81.4% of defense companies. Research indicates direct involvement in conflicts has a significant positive impact on the performance of defense stocks, but this effect is offset by the negative reaction in subsequent periods, enabling short-term hedging decisions or safe havens for investors.\n\n## Energy Sector\n\nRecent geopolitical events demonstrate strong energy sector reactions. A sudden escalation of geopolitical tensions in the Middle East sent energy prices sharply higher, and the \"winners\" are emerging from the defense and energy sectors, which thrive on the very volatility that is punishing the broader market, with Lockheed Martin and Northrop Grumman seeing share prices move inversely to the S&P 500.\n\n## Semiconductor Sector\n\nThe semiconductor sector shows vulnerability to geopolitical risks. Semiconductor stocks initially experienced volatility as geopolitical tensions weighed on sentiment, with the PHLX Semiconductor Index dropping sharply after a sell-off in Asian chip markets. However, the sector recovered later in the week, with memory producers posting strong gains as investors bought the dip.\n\n## Market Timing Patterns\n\nDefense and cybersecurity sectors act as defensive assets during geopolitical crises, while energy and critical raw materials sectors exhibit increased sensitivity to external shocks. Additionally, U.S. markets react faster to geopolitical risk than Asian markets.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"dcbbb2eb-9dea-40ce-b605-975da677e4b9","timestamp":"2026-03-08T12:41:59.579Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"2d83425d-02b1-4506-af0e-53ef1b300ac2"}

{"parentUuid":"400e5e3a-9aa7-4dd1-9014-2e33dae23e1a","isSidechain":true,"userType":"external","cwd":"C:\\Users\\baenb\\projects\\MIDGE","sessionId":"141c733c-7949-435d-8de2-34bf8e29a67f","version":"2.1.71","gitBranch":"main","agentId":"a7dea850a9df9eda1","slug":"delegated-leaping-map","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01R7bedEZVcpZRr7yXy88JHA","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"tariff announcement trade policy market signal WTO WCI trade flow data free API\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"WTO | Tariff and trade data\",\"url\":\"https://www.wto.org/english/tratop_e/tariffs_e/tariff_data_e.htm\"},{\"title\":\"WTO Statistics: Trade and tariff data\",\"url\":\"https://wto.org/statistics\"},{\"title\":\"WTO Data – Information on trade and trade policy measures\",\"url\":\"https://data.wto.org/\"},{\"title\":\"WTO Tariff and Trade Data - Official Global Trade Database\",\"url\":\"https://ttd.wto.org/en\"},{\"title\":\"Home - WTO API Developer Portal\",\"url\":\"https://apiportal.wto.org/\"},{\"title\":\"WTO | International trade and tariff data\",\"url\":\"https://www.wto.org/english/res_e/statis_e/trade_data_e.htm\"},{\"title\":\"WTO Global Trade Data Portal\",\"url\":\"https://globaltradedata.wto.org/\"},{\"title\":\"DATA SOURCES FOR TRADE POLICY ANALYSIS Arun Jacob ESCAP\",\"url\":\"https://www.unescap.org/sites/default/files/Session5-Data%20Sources%20for%20Trade%20Analysis.pdf\"},{\"title\":\"WTO Data and statistics\",\"url\":\"https://www.wto-ilibrary.org/wto-data\"},{\"title\":\"WTO | Integrated Database\",\"url\":\"https://www.wto.org/english/tratop_e/tariffs_e/idb_e.htm\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's what I found about tariff announcements, trade policy, market signals, WTO data, and free APIs:\n\n## WTO Tariff and Trade Data Resources\n\nThe WTO Analytical Database is a comprehensive tariff and trade flow database that consolidates most-favoured nation (MFN) bound, applied and preferential tariffs, together with trade flow data from various sources.\n\n### Tariff Tracker for Market Signals\n\nThe World Trade Organization (WTO) and the International Monetary Fund (IMF) have joint efforts to monitor changes in applied tariff duties. This WTO – IMF tariff tracker aims to improve transparency regarding tariff actions due to their trade and macroeconomic implications. The Tariff Tracker records initial levels and changes in effective applied duties, along with their implementation dates, at the bilateral and product levels.\n\n### API Access\n\nData can be filtered by economy, product/sector, and time period. Download data as an Excel file or csv file, or create an API account to access the data via API. Additionally, available time series cover merchandise trade and trade in services statistics (annual, quarterly and monthly), market access indicators (bound, applied & preferential tariffs), non-tariff information as well as other indicators.\n\n### Main Data Platforms\n\nThe Tariff & Trade Data platform provides official tariff and import data for more than 150 economies, including annual data from 1996 onwards for many of them. It aggregates and disseminates official information about applied tariffs and import data notified by WTO Members in the WTO's Integrated Data Base (IDB) and bound duties (agreed maximum tariffs) and other commitments recorded in the WTO's Consolidated Tariff Schedules (CTS) database.\n\nYou can access these resources through the WTO API Developer Portal and the WTO Tariff & Trade Data platform for comprehensive trade flow data and tariff information.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"49ac19db-e2bd-48a3-bc72-24f7c31b3423","timestamp":"2026-03-08T12:42:00.408Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"400e5e3a-9aa7-4dd1-9014-2e33dae23e1a"}

    1→#!/usr/bin/env python3\n     2→\"\"\"\n     3→Convergence Alerter - Generates alerts when patterns converge across domains.\n     4→\n     5→CORE OUTPUT for trading decisions:\n     6→- Detects when multiple signals from different domains align\n     7→- \"crypto whale + congress trade + hiring surge = actionable signal\"\n     8→- Combines velocity, correlation, and confidence into single alert\n     9→\n    10→Usage:\n    11→    from mae_core.market.intelligence.convergence_alerter import ConvergenceAlerter\n    12→\n    13→    alerter = ConvergenceAlerter()\n    14→    alerter.record_signal(\"insider_buys\", 0.8, \"insider\", direction=\"bullish\")\n    15→    alerter.record_signal(\"crypto_whales\", 0.7, \"crypto\", direction=\"bullish\")\n    16→\n    17→    alerts = alerter.check_convergence()\n    18→    for alert in alerts:\n    19→        print(alert.summary)\n    20→\"\"\"\n    21→\n    22→import json\n    23→import logging\n    24→import math\n    25→import os\n    26→import threading\n    27→import time\n    28→from dataclasses import dataclass, field\n    29→from datetime import datetime, timedelta\n    30→from pathlib import Path\n    31→from typing import Dict, List, Optional\n    32→from collections import defaultdict\n    33→\n    34→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n    35→\n    36→# Discovery log path — same resolution as When 3+ domains\n   111→    from different categories all point the same direction, that's\n   112→    a much stronger signal.\n   113→\n   114→    Example convergence:\n   115→    - Insider domain: Executives buying (bullish)\n   116→    - Crypto domain: Whales accumulating (bullish)\n   117→    - Government domain: Contract awarded (bullish)\n   118→    → CONVERGENCE ALERT: 3 domains bullish = high confidence\n   119→\n   120→    Confidence is computed via Thompson-weighted geometric mean when\n   121→    a ThompsonSampler is provided. These are signals\n    11→nobody else cross-references with insider trades and congressional activity.\n    12→\n    13→Key data:\n    14→  - Weekly Petroleum Status Report (every Wednesday 10:30 AM ET)\n    15→  - Weekly Natural Gas Storage Report (every Thursday 10:30 AM ET)\n    16→  - Monthly production data\n    17→\n    18→API base: https://api.eia.gov/v2\n    19→Auth: ?api_key={KEY} (query parameter)\n    20→Response: {\"response\": {\"data\": [...]}}\n    21→\"\"\"\n    22→\n    23→import os\n    24→import time\n    25→import logging\n    26→from dataclasses import dataclass, field\n    27→from datetime import datetime, timedelta\n    28→from typing import List, Optional, Dict, Any\n    29→import requests\n    30→\n    31→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n    32→\n    33→EIA_BASE_URL = \"https://api.eia.gov/v2\"\n    34→\n    35→# Rate limiting — conservative to stay well under ~9,000/hour\n    36→REQUEST_DELAY = 0.7  #
