{"parentUuid":"b6867104-0f80-4474-853d-d3d8f66deee2","isSidechain":true,"userType":"external","cwd":"C:\\Users\\baenb\\projects\\MIDGE","sessionId":"141c733c-7949-435d-8de2-34bf8e29a67f","version":"2.1.71","gitBranch":"main","agentId":"a7dea850a9df9eda1","slug":"delegated-leaping-map","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-4-6","id":"msg_01PjWSVRVgSRWbaQNRm8KN15","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Now I have everything needed to produce a comprehensive, architecture-grounded research deliverable.\n\n---\n\n# Geopolitical Intelligence as a First-Class MIDGE Domain\n\n## Executive Summary\n\nMIDGE currently has a blind spot the size of the world stage. Every domain in the current stack — insider trades, congressional activity, contracts, macro data, technicals — reflects events *after* geopolitical forces have already set them in motion. Adding a `geopolitical` domain closes the loop: MIDGE gains early-warning signals that precede the insider buys, the defense contracts, and the energy inventory draws by days to weeks.\n\nThis research covers seven concrete source bundles, their APIs, integration points in MIDGE's architecture, and the temporal lead structure that makes geopolitical signals uniquely valuable for pattern stacking.\n\n---\n\n## Part 1: The Temporal Lead Thesis\n\nThe core value proposition of geopolitical signals is that they fire *before* the market's other reaction layers. Research establishes this rough sequencing:\n\n| Stage | What fires | Approximate timing |\n|---|---|---|\n| 0 | Geopolitical event / threat escalation | T+0 |\n| 1 | Commodity prices move (oil, gold, nat gas) | T+0 to T+3 days |\n| 2 | Defense/energy/shipping stocks diverge from index | T+1 to T+7 days |\n| 3 | Congressional members trade on committee intel | T+7 to T+30 days |\n| 4 | Defense contract awards follow spending bills | T+30 to T+90 days |\n| 5 | Corporate earnings/guidance reflect supply chain reality | T+60 to T+180 days |\n\nMIDGE already captures Stages 3-5 (congressional, contracts, insider). Adding a geopolitical layer captures Stage 0-2 — the causal root — and allows the convergence engine to detect when the geopolitical trigger *preceded* the downstream signals already in the buffer. This upgrades from \"coincidence detection\" to \"causal chain detection.\"\n\nThe `sequence_score` field already exists on `ConvergenceAlert` for this exact purpose. A geopolitical signal arriving before insider and defense contract signals in a known pattern should boost `sequence_score` above 1.0.\n\n---\n\n## Part 2: Source Bundle A — GDELT (Primary Free Source)\n\n**What it is:** The Global Database of Events, Language, and Tone. Monitors every broadcast, print, and web news source globally. 15-minute updates. Free, no API key required. 47 years of history.\n\n**Python package:** `gdeltdoc` (PyPI) — wraps the GDELT 2.0 Doc API. Install: `pip install gdeltdoc`\n\n**Key signal types available:**\n\n1. **Event tone scores** — Average tone across all articles about an event (range roughly -10 to +10). Strongly negative tone + high volume = escalation signal.\n\n2. **CAMEO event codes** — 300+ hierarchically coded event types. Market-relevant clusters:\n   - `14*` = Protest/demonstrate\n   - `15*` = Force posture (military movement)\n   - `16*` = Reduce relations (sanctions, expulsions)\n   - `17*` = Coerce (threaten, pressure)\n   - `18*` = Assault (armed attack)\n   - `19*` = Fight\n   - `20*` = Use unconventional mass violence\n\n3. **Goldstein Scale** — Numeric conflict-cooperation score per event type, from -10 (most hostile) to +10 (most cooperative). Directly usable as a signal strength value.\n\n4. **GKG (Global Knowledge Graph) themes** — 2,500+ named themes filterable per article. Geopolitically relevant ones:\n   - `WB_693` — Sanctions\n   - `MILITARY` — Military activity\n   - `CRISISLEX_C03_TERRORISM` — Terrorism events\n   - `TAX_FREETR_TRADE` — Free trade/tariff themes\n   - `EMBARGO` — Trade embargo\n   - Full list: github.com/CatoMinor/GDELT-GKG-Themes\n\n**Update frequency:** Every 15 minutes. New CSV files drop at: `http://data.gdeltproject.org/gdeltv2/lastupdate.txt`\n\n**How to use in MIDGE:**\n\n```python\n# Proposed: mae_core/market/apis/gdelt_client.py\nfrom gdeltdoc import GdeltDoc, Filters\n\ngd = GdeltDoc()\n# Example: Russia-Ukraine military conflict mentions last 24h\nf = Filters(\n    keyword=\"military escalation OR sanctions OR conflict\",\n    start_date=\"2026-03-07\",\n    end_date=\"2026-03-08\",\n    theme=\"WB_693\"  # Sanctions theme\n)\narticles = gd.article_search(f)\ntimeline = gd.timeline_search(\"timelinetone\", f)\n# timeline[\"timeline\"] = list of (datetime, avg_tone) pairs\n# Derive: tone_delta = current_avg - 7d_avg → signal strength\n```\n\n**Signal derivation for MIDGE:**\n- Compute 7-day rolling average tone for a country/region\n- When tone drops more than 1.5 standard deviations below baseline: bearish geopolitical signal for exposed sectors\n- When CAMEO code 15* (military posture) frequency spikes: flag affected tickers via `WorldModel.find_ripple_effects(\"geopolitical_tension_escalation\")`\n\n**Thompson key:** `gdelt_tone` — starts at Beta(2, 2), learns as outcomes come in\n\n---\n\n## Part 3: Source Bundle B — GPR Index (Caldara-Iacoviello)\n\n**What it is:** The academic gold standard for geopolitical risk measurement. Built by Federal Reserve economists. Counts newspaper articles using geopolitical risk keywords across 10 major papers. Updated monthly. Free download.\n\n**Data format:** CSV/Excel download from `https://www.matteoiacoviello.com/gpr.htm`\n\n**What's available:**\n- Aggregate GPR index (1985-present, monthly)\n- Country-specific GPR indices for 46 countries\n- Geopolitical Acts vs. Threats split (threats often precede acts — this is the leading indicator component)\n- \"Acts\" subindex (military clashes, terrorist attacks) vs. \"Threats\" subindex (political tensions, sanctions threats)\n\n**Why the Threats/Acts split matters:** The Threats subindex leads the Acts subindex by roughly 30-60 days. When threats spike, acts tend to follow. This is a pure leading indicator within the leading indicator.\n\n**FRED availability:** The GPR index is available on FRED as series `GPRI` (monthly). MIDGE already has a `fred_client.py`. This is a zero-new-client addition — just add the series to the existing FRED client.\n\n**Signal integration:**\n- Monthly GPR spike > 2σ above baseline → register `geopolitical` domain signal, bearish for equities broadly, bullish for gold (GLD, GDX), bullish for defense (LMT, RTX, NOC, GD, BA)\n- Country-specific GPR → weight by ticker's geographic revenue exposure (see Part 6)\n- GPR Threats subindex rising faster than Acts → early warning, 30-60 day lead\n\n---\n\n## Part 4: Source Bundle C — OpenSanctions + OFAC SDN\n\n**What it is:** OpenSanctions aggregates 269 sanctions datasets globally — OFAC, EU, UN, UK, Australia — into a unified, searchable database. The raw OFAC SDN list is also directly downloadable from Treasury.\n\n**OpenSanctions API:**\n- Base URL: `https://api.opensanctions.org/`\n- Free for non-commercial use (CC 4.0 Attribution-NonCommercial)\n- Endpoints: entity search, dataset metadata, bulk dataset download\n- 75,746 sanctioned entities across 269 datasets\n- Python package: `pip install opensanctions` (version 3.2.0 on PyPI)\n\n**OFAC SDN direct monitoring:**\n- Raw XML: `https://www.treasury.gov/ofac/downloads/sdn.xml` (updated same-day as changes)\n- OFAC email notification service for list updates (free, subscribe at OFAC website)\n- GitHub project `jeffallen13/ofac` tracks monthly OFAC data as a panel dataset with Python scripts\n\n**Why this is a leading indicator:** OFAC sanctions are not announced — they are imposed. When a new country, company, or individual appears on the SDN list, the financial consequences (asset freezes, transaction prohibitions) take effect immediately. New entity additions are:\n- Bearish for companies doing business with the newly sanctioned entity\n- Bullish for competitors who gain market share\n- A leading indicator of broader geopolitical deterioration\n\n**Signal derivation:**\n- Daily diff of SDN XML against prior version\n- New additions: flag by country/sector → run `WorldModel.find_ripple_effects(\"russia_sanctions\")` or equivalent\n- New **country-level** additions (e.g., all entities in a country) = severe signal, escalate urgency\n\n**Thompson key:** `ofac_sanctions` — high prior reliability given the legal certainty of sanctions\n\n---\n\n## Part 5: Source Bundle D — ACLED (Armed Conflict Location and Event Data)\n\n**What it is:** Academic-grade conflict event database. Real data on protests, riots, battles, explosions, violence. Covers every country. Updated weekly (real-time in conflict zones).\n\n**Access:** Free registration at acleddata.com. API key provided. Python package: `pip install acled`\n\n**Key fields:**\n- `event_type`: Battle / Explosions/Remote violence / Protests / Riots / Strategic developments\n- `sub_event_type`: Armed clash, Air/drone strike, Protest with intervention, etc.\n- `fatalities`: Count\n- `country`, `admin1`, `admin2`, `location`: Geographic precision\n- `actor1`, `actor2`: Named parties\n\n**Signal derivation for MIDGE:**\n- Count of armed conflict events per country in last 7 days vs. 90-day baseline\n- Z-score > 2: elevated instability signal\n- Map country → affected sectors via supply chain exposure database (see Part 6)\n- Fatality spike in oil-producing region → bearish energy supply signal, wires into EIA domain\n\n**Rate limit:** Standard API, no stated limit but research-oriented — reasonable polling (daily fetch fine)\n\n**Thompson key:** `acled_conflict` — start at Beta(2, 3) (slightly negative prior; most conflict stays localized)\n\n---\n\n## Part 6: Source Bundle E — Supply Chain Geography Layer (Enabling Infrastructure)\n\nThis is the *translation layer* that converts country-level events into ticker-level signals. Without it, \"Russia escalation\" produces a vague market signal. With it, MIDGE knows that NVDA has 0% Russia exposure but 30% China manufacturing exposure, making it specifically sensitive to Taiwan Strait signals, not Russia signals.\n\n**Three data sources to build this layer:**\n\n**1. SEC XBRL Geographic Revenue Segments (free)**\n- `data.sec.gov/api/xbrl/companyfacts/CIK{cik}.json`\n- Contains `RevenueFromContractWithCustomerExcludingAssessedTax` broken down by geographic segment where companies report it\n- ~40-50% of S&P 500 companies report country-level breakdown in their XBRL filings\n- MIDGE already has an EDGAR client — this is a new method on the existing client\n\n**2. UN Comtrade (free tier)**\n- Python package: `pip install comtradeapicall`\n- Rate limit: 100 requests/hour on free tier\n- Use case: Map US import dependency by HS product code × country\n  - Taiwan: semiconductors (HS 8542)\n  - China: rare earths (HS 2805), consumer electronics (HS 8517)\n  - Saudi Arabia/UAE: crude oil (HS 2709)\n  - Russia: palladium (HS 7110), neon gas (HS 2804)\n- Build a static `COUNTRY_SECTOR_DEPENDENCY` dict that maps `(country, sector)` → affected tickers\n\n**3. `country-converter` (coco) (free)**\n- `pip install country-converter`\n- Converts country names/codes between ISO, FIPS, UN, region, continent\n- Essential for joining GDELT/ACLED data (which uses varied country name formats) with SEC/Comtrade data\n\n**Proposed static dependency map (hardcoded starting point, Granger-refined over time):**\n\n```python\nCOUNTRY_SECTOR_DEPENDENCY = {\n    (\"Taiwan\", \"semiconductors\"): [\"NVDA\", \"AMD\", \"INTC\", \"AVGO\", \"QCOM\", \"TSM\"],\n    (\"China\", \"rare_earths\"): [\"MP\", \"ARNC\", \"defense_broadly\"],\n    (\"China\", \"manufacturing\"): [\"AAPL\", \"consumer_electronics_broadly\"],\n    (\"Russia\", \"energy\"): [\"XOM\", \"CVX\", \"BP\", \"SHEL\", nat_gas_broadly],\n    (\"Russia\", \"metals\"): [\"palladium_futures\", \"steel_broadly\"],\n    (\"Saudi_Arabia\", \"energy\"): [\"XOM\", \"CVX\", \"USO\", \"CL=F\"],\n    (\"Middle_East\", \"shipping\"): [\"ZIM\", \"MATX\", \"shipping_broadly\"],\n    (\"Ukraine\", \"agriculture\"): [\"wheat_futures\", \"DBA\", \"ADM\", \"BG\"],\n    (\"Iran\", \"energy\"): [\"USO\", \"CL=F\"],\n    (\"North_Korea\", \"defense\"): [\"LMT\", \"RTX\", \"NOC\", \"GD\"],\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## Part 7: Source Bundle F — Trade Policy and Tariffs\n\n**WTO API Developer Portal:**\n- API at `https://apiportal.wto.org/` (free registration)\n- The WTO-IMF Tariff Tracker records tariff changes, implementation dates, bilateral/product level\n- Endpoint: tariff rate changes by country pair and HS code\n\n**Signal derivation:**\n- New tariff > 10% on a major product category: bearish for importers, bullish for domestic producers\n- Tariff escalation ladder (threat → provisional → confirmed): temporal sequencing, each step is a new signal\n\n**Congressional Research Service (CRS) Reports:**\n- Free RSS feed at `https://crsreports.congress.gov/`\n- CRS publishes reports on every major geopolitical/trade topic; these often precede policy action by weeks\n\n**Thompson key:** `trade_policy_tariff` — start at Beta(2, 2)\n\n---\n\n## Part 8: Source Bundle G — WTI/Commodity Geopolitical Pressure Coefficient\n\nRather than a separate data source, this is a *synthesis signal* that MIDGE can derive from existing sources it already has. The logic:\n\nWhen the following all fire within 7 days:\n1. EIA crude inventory draw (already captured, `eia_energy` domain)\n2. ACLED conflict escalation in oil-producing region\n3. GPR index spike\n4. VIX term structure inversion (already captured, `vix_term_structure` domain)\n\n...the convergence engine fires with `geopolitical + energy + volatility` as the domain stack. This is the Russia-Ukraine oil shock pattern in template form.\n\nThe existing `WorldModel` already has edges for `russia_sanctions → energy_supply_disruption_eu → nat_gas_spike`. Adding geopolitical data sources makes those edges *fireable* rather than merely decorative.\n\n---\n\n## Part 9: NLP Approach for Geopolitical Text\n\nFor sources that return raw text (news articles, CRS reports, press releases), MIDGE needs a lightweight NLP pipeline to extract a scalar signal. Recommended approach matching MIDGE's existing `social_text_analyzer.py` pattern:\n\n**Escalation keyword tiers** (convert text to Goldstein-like scalar):\n\n```python\nGEOPOLITICAL_ESCALATION_KEYWORDS = {\n    # Tier 3 — severe (weight 1.0)\n    \"severe\": [\"invasion\", \"military strike\", \"nuclear\", \"sanctions imposed\",\n               \"blockade\", \"embargo\", \"seized\", \"airstrikes\", \"missile launch\"],\n    # Tier 2 — significant (weight 0.6)\n    \"significant\": [\"sanctions warning\", \"troop deployment\", \"naval exercise\",\n                    \"diplomatic expulsion\", \"trade restriction\", \"tariff imposed\",\n                    \"military buildup\", \"economic coercion\"],\n    # Tier 1 — elevated (weight 0.3)\n    \"elevated\": [\"tensions rising\", \"diplomatic warning\", \"trade dispute\",\n                 \"sanctions threat\", \"military drills\", \"border incident\"],\n}\n```\n\nThis matches the pattern already in `social_text_analyzer.py` and needs no external NLP library (no spaCy, no transformers — just keyword matching with regex, matching MIDGE's philosophy of lightweight, auditable signal extraction).\n\n---\n\n## Part 10: Integration Architecture\n\n**New domain name:** `geopolitical`\n\n**New files to create:**\n\n| File | Purpose |\n|---|---|\n| `mae_core/market/apis/gdelt_client.py` | GDELT DOC API fetcher, tone/event queries |\n| `mae_core/market/apis/ofac_client.py` | OFAC SDN XML diff monitor |\n| `mae_core/market/apis/acled_client.py` | ACLED conflict event fetcher |\n| `mae_core/market/apis/geopolitical_country_map.py` | Static `COUNTRY_SECTOR_DEPENDENCY` dict + lookup functions |\n\n**Changes to existing files:**\n\n1. `mae_core/market/archaeology/pattern_library.py` — add to `_SOURCE_DOMAIN_MAP`:\n   ```python\n   \"gdelt_tone\": \"geopolitical\",\n   \"ofac_sanctions\": \"geopolitical\",\n   \"acled_conflict\": \"geopolitical\",\n   \"gpr_index\": \"geopolitical\",\n   \"trade_policy_tariff\": \"geopolitical\",\n   ```\n\n2. `mae_core/market/intelligence/learning_config.py` — add to `decay_rates`:\n   ```python\n   \"geopolitical\": 0.08,  # Half-life ~9 days — escalations unfold over weeks, resolve slowly\n   ```\n\n3. `mae_core/market/intelligence/convergence_alerter.py` — add to `_SOURCE_TO_THOMPSON_KEY`:\n   ```python\n   \"gdelt_tone\": \"gdelt_tone\",\n   \"ofac_sanctions\": \"ofac_sanctions\",\n   \"acled_conflict\": \"acled_conflict\",\n   \"gpr_index\": \"gpr_index\",\n   ```\n\n4. `mae_core/market/intelligence/world_model.py` — expand the existing `GEOPOLITICAL` section with Taiwan/Iran/OPEC chains currently missing.\n\n5. `mae_core/market/sensing_fetchers.py` — add `fetch_gdelt()`, `fetch_ofac_diff()`, `fetch_acled()` functions following the existing pattern.\n\n6. `mae_core/market/intelligence/thompson_sampler.py` — add new distribution keys for the 5 geopolitical sources.\n\n---\n\n## Part 11: Temporal Lead Exploitation in Convergence Engine\n\nThe `sequence_score` on `ConvergenceAlert` already exists but currently has no geopolitical layer to exploit. Once geopolitical signals are live, the expected temporal sequences to encode:\n\n```python\n# In convergence_alerter.py KNOWN_SEQUENCES dict (if it exists) or WorldModel\nGEOPOLITICAL_LEAD_SEQUENCES = {\n    # Geopolitical fires first → then energy → sequence_score boost\n    (\"geopolitical\", \"energy\", \"technical\"):        1.25,  # escalation → oil → chart breakout\n    (\"geopolitical\", \"volatility\", \"technical\"):    1.20,  # fear spike → VIX → technical breakdown\n    (\"geopolitical\", \"government\", \"contracts\"):    1.15,  # conflict → congressional → defense contracts\n    (\"geopolitical\", \"institutional\", \"insider\"):   1.10,  # sanctions → institutions reposition → insiders follow\n    # Reversed (technical fires before geopolitical) → slight discount\n    (\"technical\", \"geopolitical\"):                  0.90,  # TA leading geo is unusual, discount\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## Part 12: Key Historical Examples as Archaeology Templates\n\nThese should be seeded as pre-validated `PatternTemplate` instances in the archaeology library (no excavation needed — they are historically documented):\n\n| Event | Pre-signal domain stack | Lead time | Instruments |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| Russia-Ukraine invasion (Feb 2022) | geopolitical(threats) + government(sanctions signals) + energy(supply concern) | 2-3 weeks of threat escalation | XOM, CVX, XLE, GLD, nat gas |\n| China-Taiwan tensions (Aug 2022 Pelosi visit) | geopolitical(military exercises) + technical(TSMC breakdowns) | 7-14 days | TSM, NVDA, AMD, SOXX |\n| Suez Canal blockage (Mar 2021) | geopolitical(shipping disruption) + technical(shipping indices) | 0 (simultaneous) | ZIM, MATX, XOM (Suez rerouting adds oil demand) |\n| OPEC+ surprise cut (Oct 2022) | geopolitical(Saudi-US friction) + energy(production data) | 3-7 days before announcement | USO, XLE, XOM |\n| Silicon Valley Bank collapse + geopolitical (Mar 2023) | geopolitical(de-dollarization discussion) + macro(rate stress) | coincident | TLT, GLD, crypto |\n\n---\n\n## Part 13: Implementation Priority\n\n**Phase 1 (highest leverage, lowest cost):**\n1. Add GPR index to existing `fred_client.py` — zero new client, just a new FRED series ID. Immediate value.\n2. Create `gdelt_client.py` using `gdeltdoc` (pip install, no API key). Tone + CAMEO code filtering. Wired as 15-minute-resolution global event feed.\n3. Add `geopolitical` domain to `_SOURCE_DOMAIN_MAP`, `decay_rates`, and Thompson sampler.\n\n**Phase 2:**\n4. Create `ofac_client.py` — daily XML diff monitor. High signal-to-noise on new additions.\n5. Create `geopolitical_country_map.py` — static dependency dict. Enables ticker routing for geo signals.\n6. Expand `WorldModel` geopolitical section with Taiwan/Iran/OPEC/shipping chains.\n\n**Phase 3:**\n7. `acled_client.py` — requires free ACLED account registration.\n8. UN Comtrade integration for dynamic country-sector dependency updates.\n9. Granger causality testing between GPR index and existing Thompson signals (30-day lag candidates).\n\n**Free / no key required:** GDELT, GPR (download), OFAC SDN XML, WorldModel expansion, geopolitical_country_map\n**Requires registration (free):** ACLED (free academic account), WTO API (free registration), UN Comtrade (free tier)\n\n---\n\n## Summary of New Python Dependencies\n\n```\ngdeltdoc>=1.12.0       # GDELT Doc API client\nacled>=0.1             # ACLED conflict data\ncomtradeapicall>=0.1   # UN Comtrade\ncountry-converter>=0.8 # Country code normalization\n```\n\nNo NLP libraries required — the keyword-tier approach in Part 9 uses only Python builtins and matches MIDGE's existing `social_text_analyzer.py` pattern.\n\n---\n\nSources:\n- [GDELT Project Data](https://www.gdeltproject.org/data.html)\n- [gdelt-doc-api Python Client](https://github.com/alex9smith/gdelt-doc-api)\n- [gdeltdoc PyPI](https://pypi.org/project/gdeltdoc/)\n- [ACLED Data](https://acleddata.com/)\n- [acled PyPI](https://pypi.org/project/acled/)\n- [OpenSanctions API](https://www.opensanctions.org/api/)\n- [OFAC Sanctions List](https://ofac.treasury.gov/sanctions-list-service)\n- [GPR Index -
